I just feel like I've got this motor humming right under my skin whenever I'm supposed to sit still. \n\nLike, if I'm in a staff meeting at work, or sitting through church event briefings, I just can't stay put. It's really starting to become a problem for me.\n\nDoctor: Does this restlessness happen only in situations where you're supposed to sit still, or does it occur at other times as well?\n\nPatient: No, it's pretty much all the time. From the dialogue so far and your current candidates, ask the one follow-up question that best separates them.\n\n[Current Turn]\n8\n\n[Current Candidates]\nF90.0, F90.8, F90.9\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi, doctor. Well, what brings me in is... I just feel like I've got this motor humming right under my skin whenever I'm supposed to sit still. \n\nLike, if I'm in a staff meeting at work, or sitting through church event briefings, I just can't stay put. I always end up shifting around in my chair, standing up, or just trying to find some excuse to get up and move around. I remember getting in trouble back in grade school quite a bit for it, actually. I was always popping out of my seat, or... well, talking over the teacher. I think I know what they're going to say, so I blurt it out before they can even finish their sentence. The problem is, half the time I get it wrong, and then they have to stop and correct me. It's frustrating for them, and it's frustrating for me, but I just can't seem to hold it in.\n\nDoctor: Do you often intrude on others or interrupt their activities?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so. I mean, I know I interrupt when people are talking, like I just mentioned, but I don't usually barge in on what they're actually doing. \n\nAt work, I'm mostly just doing my own thing, cleaning and moving around, so I don't really get in anyone's way or take over their tasks. And at home I'm usually out in the garage by myself. So, no, I haven't really noticed myself doing that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been known to act impulsively, like doing something without thinking it through, outside of interrupting or blurting out answers?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, aside from the talking part we just went over, I can't really think of anything. I don't really make rash decisions or just... jump into doing things out of the blue without thinking. I pretty much just stick to my routine cleaning the building at work, and then I go home. So, no, I wouldn't say I act impulsively in other ways.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty paying attention to details or making careless mistakes in your work or other activities?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My job is pretty straightforward, you know? Just cleaning and keeping the building in order. I've got my routine down, so I don't really miss things or make careless mistakes. So it's definitely not a new thing.\n\nDoctor: Did you often interrupt others or blurt out answers before they finished speaking when you were in school?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I did, and honestly, I still do. It happens a lot at work or when I'm volunteering at church. When my supervisor or one of the church staff starts giving me instructions, I just... jump in. I think I know what they're going to say, so I blurt it out before they can even finish their sentence. The problem is, half the time I get it wrong, and then they have to stop and correct me. It's frustrating for them, and it's frustrating for me, but I just can't seem to hold it in.\n\nDoctor: Do you often intrude on others or interrupt their activities?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so. I mean, I know I interrupt when people are talking, like I just mentioned, but I don't usually barge in on what they're actually doing. \n\nAt work, I'm mostly just doing my own thing, cleaning and moving around, so I don't really get in anyone's way or take over their tasks. And at home I'm usually out in the garage by myself. So, no, I haven't really noticed myself doing that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been known to act impulsively, like doing something without thinking it through, outside of interrupting or blurting out answers?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, aside from the talking part we just went over, I can't really think of anything. I don't really make rash decisions or just... jump into doing things out of the blue without thinking. I pretty much just stick to my routine cleaning the building at work, and then I go home. So, no, I wouldn't say I act impulsively in other ways.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty paying attention to details or making careless mistakes in your work or other activities?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My job is pretty straightforward, you know? Just cleaning and keeping the building in order. I've got my routine down, so I don't really miss things or make careless mistakes. I just do what needs to be done, so... yeah, missing details isn't really a problem for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble organizing tasks and activities?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I have trouble with that. Like I said, my job is pretty routine. I know what I need to do every day—empty the trash, mop the floors, check the bathrooms—and I just do it. I don't really have to plan out big projects or organize much. I just go down my list and get it done. So, organizing hasn't really been an issue for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often talk excessively, even when it's not your turn or when others are trying to converse?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I talk excessively. I know we talked earlier about how I can sometimes blurt things out or interrupt, but I don't just go on and on. Mostly I'm pretty quiet. I just keep my head down and do my cleaning at work, so I'm not really talking people's ears off or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms, such as restlessness and interrupting others, caused you any significant problems in your relationships or at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it actually has at work. Um... I've started getting some complaints and corrections lately. I guess I end up missing parts of instructions when people are talking to me. And it creates some friction, you know, especially during room turnovers or when we're trying to make decisions about supplies. It actually got to the point where my supervisor pushed me toward the employee assistance program. So... yeah, it's definitely causing some issues on the job.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."

I think I know what they're going to say, so I blurt it out before they can even finish their sentence. The problem is, half the time I get it wrong, and then they have to stop and correct me. It's frustrating for them, and it's frustrating for me, but I just can't seem to hold it in.\n\nDoctor: Do you often intrude on others or interrupt their activities?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so. I mean, I know I interrupt when people are talking, like I just mentioned, but I don't usually barge in on what they're actually doing. \n\nAt work, I'm mostly just doing my own thing, cleaning and moving around, so I don't really get in anyone's way or take over their tasks. And at home I'm usually out in the garage by myself. So, no, I haven't really noticed myself doing that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been known to act impulsively, like doing something without thinking it through, outside of interrupting or blurting out answers?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, aside from the talking part we just went over, I can't really think of anything. I don't really make rash decisions or just... jump into doing things out of the blue without thinking. I pretty much just stick to my routine cleaning the building at work, and then I go home. So, no, I wouldn't say I act impulsively in other ways.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty paying attention to details or making careless mistakes in your work or other activities?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My job is pretty straightforward, you know? Just cleaning and keeping the building in order. I've got my routine down, so I don't really miss things or make careless mistakes. I just do what needs to be done, so... yeah, missing details isn't really a problem for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble organizing tasks and activities?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I have trouble with that. Like I said, my job is pretty routine. I know what I need to do every day—empty the trash, mop the floors, check the bathrooms—and I just do it. I don't really have to plan out big projects or organize much. I just go down my list and get it done. So, organizing hasn't really been an issue for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often talk excessively, even when it's not your turn or when others are trying to converse?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I talk excessively. I know we talked earlier about how I can sometimes blurt things out or interrupt, but I don't just go on and on. Mostly I'm pretty quiet. I just keep my head down and do my cleaning at work, so I'm not really talking people's ears off or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms, such as restlessness and interrupting others, caused you any significant problems in your relationships or at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it actually has at work. Um... I've started getting some complaints and corrections lately. I guess I end up missing parts of instructions when people are talking to me. And it creates some friction, you know, especially during room turnovers or when we're trying to make decisions about supplies. It actually got to the point where my supervisor pushed me toward the employee assistance program. So... yeah, it's definitely causing some issues on the job.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms, such as restlessness and interrupting others, caused you any significant problems in your personal relationships?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Um, my personal life is pretty quiet. I don't really have any big issues with family or friends over it. I guess the people who know me are just kind of used to how I am by now. So, no, it hasn't really caused any major problems outside of work.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n13\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF90.0, F90.8, F90.9\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi, doctor. Well, what brings me in is... I just feel like I've got this motor humming right under my skin whenever I'm supposed to sit still. \n\nLike, if I'm in a staff meeting at work, or sitting through church event briefings, I just can't stay put. I always end up shifting around in my chair, standing up, or just trying to find some excuse to get up and move around. It's really starting to become a problem for me.\n\nDoctor: Does this restlessness happen only in situations where you're supposed to sit still, or does it occur at other times as well?\n\nPatient: No, it's pretty much all the time. Like, on my shifts—I work as a janitor—I just go from mopping to checking the trash to straightening chairs without really stopping. I just keep moving. \n\nAnd even at home, when you'd think I'd want to rest my feet... I'll get back and just start puttering around in the garage, or I'll be re-sorting my tools instead of actually sitting down after work. It's just hard to turn it off.\n\nDoctor: Did you have trouble sitting still or felt overly restless when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I did. I remember getting in trouble back in grade school quite a bit for it, actually. I was always popping out of my seat, or... well, talking over the teacher. I'd usually just rush ahead and start on things before the directions were even finished. My mother, she used to say I acted like I was wound too tight, even as a kid. So it's definitely not a new thing.\n\nDoctor: Did you often interrupt others or blurt out answers before they finished speaking when you were in school?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I did, and honestly, I still do. It happens a lot at work or when I'm volunteering at church. When my supervisor or one of the church staff starts giving me instructions, I just... jump in. I think I know what they're going to say, so I blurt it out before they can even finish their sentence. The problem is, half the time I get it wrong, and then they have to stop and correct me. It's frustrating for them, and it's frustrating for me, but I just can't seem to hold it in.\n\nDoctor: Do you often intrude on others or interrupt their activities?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so. I mean, I know I interrupt when people are talking, like I just mentioned, but I don't usually barge in on what they're actually doing. \n\nAt work, I'm mostly just doing my own thing, cleaning and moving around, so I don't really get in anyone's way or take over their tasks. And at home I'm usually out in the garage by myself. So, no, I haven't really noticed myself doing that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been known to act impulsively, like doing something without thinking it through, outside of interrupting or blurting out answers?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, aside from the talking part we just went over, I can't really think of anything. I don't really make rash decisions or just... jump into doing things out of the blue without thinking. I pretty much just stick to my routine cleaning the building at work, and then I go home. So, no, I wouldn't say I act impulsively in other ways.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty paying attention to details or making careless mistakes in your work or other activities?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My job is pretty straightforward, you know? Just cleaning and keeping the building in order. I've got my routine down, so I don't really miss things or make careless mistakes. I just do what needs to be done, so... yeah, missing details isn't really a problem for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble organizing tasks and activities?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I have trouble with that. Like I said, my job is pretty routine. I know what I need to do every day—empty the trash, mop the floors, check the bathrooms—and I just do it. I don't really have to plan out big projects or organize much. I just go down my list and get it done. So, organizing hasn't really been an issue for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often talk excessively, even when it's not your turn or when others are trying to converse?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I talk excessively. I know we talked earlier about how I can sometimes blurt things out or interrupt, but I don't just go on and on. Mostly I'm pretty quiet. I just keep my head down and do my cleaning at work, so I'm not really talking people's ears off or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms, such as restlessness and interrupting others, caused you any significant problems in your relationships or at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it actually has at work. Um... I've started getting some complaints and corrections lately. I guess I end up missing parts of instructions when people are talking to me. And it creates some friction, you know, especially during room turnovers or when we're trying to make decisions about supplies. It actually got to the point where my supervisor pushed me toward the employee assistance program. So... yeah, it's definitely causing some issues on the job.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms, such as restlessness and interrupting others, caused you any significant problems in your personal relationships?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Um, my personal life is pretty quiet. I don't really have any big issues with family or friends over it. I guess the people who know me are just kind of used to how I am by now. So, no, it hasn't really caused any major problems outside of work.\n\nDoctor: As a child, were you often described as being physically overactive, such as running, climbing, or being unable to play quietly?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I was. Um, I remember getting in trouble back in grade school for just popping out of my seat or talking over the teacher. I'd always be rushing ahead before the directions were even finished. My mother actually used to say I acted like I was wound too tight, even as a kid. So, yeah, that definitely sounds like me back then.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your restlessness or interrupting has caused you a lot of distress or anxiety?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, it gets frustrating at work when I mess up or people get annoyed with me, but I wouldn't say it causes me a lot of distress or anxiety. It's just... kind of how I've always been, so I don't really stress over it too much.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi, doctor. Well, what brings me in is... I just feel like I've got this motor humming right under my skin whenever I'm supposed to sit still. \n\nLike, if I'm in a staff meeting at work, or sitting through church event briefings, I just can't stay put. I always end up shifting around in my chair, standing up, or just trying to find some excuse to get up and move around. It's really starting to become a problem for me.\n\nDoctor: Does this restlessness happen only in situations where you're supposed to sit still, or does it occur at other times as well?\n\nPatient: No, it's pretty much all the time. Like, on my shifts—I work as a janitor—I just go from mopping to checking the trash to straightening chairs without really stopping. I just keep moving. \n\nAnd even at home, when you'd think I'd want to rest my feet... I'll get back and just start puttering around in the garage, or I'll be re-sorting my tools instead of actually sitting down after work. It's just hard to turn it off.\n\nDoctor: Did you have trouble sitting still or felt overly restless when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I did. I remember getting in trouble back in grade school quite a bit for it, actually. I was always popping out of my seat, or... well, talking over the teacher. I'd usually just rush ahead and start on things before the directions were even finished. My mother, she used to say I acted like I was wound too tight, even as a kid. So it's definitely not a new thing.\n\nDoctor: Did you often interrupt others or blurt out answers before they finished speaking when you were in school?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I did, and honestly, I still do. It happens a lot at work or when I'm volunteering at church. When my supervisor or one of the church staff starts giving me instructions, I just... jump in. I think I know what they're going to say, so I blurt it out before they can even finish their sentence. The problem is, half the time I get it wrong, and then they have to stop and correct me. It's frustrating for them, and it's frustrating for me, but I just can't seem to hold it in.\n\nDoctor: Do you often intrude on others or interrupt their activities?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so. I mean, I know I interrupt when people are talking, like I just mentioned, but I don't usually barge in on what they're actually doing. \n\nAt work, I'm mostly just doing my own thing, cleaning and moving around, so I don't really get in anyone's way or take over their tasks. And at home I'm usually out in the garage by myself. So, no, I haven't really noticed myself doing that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been known to act impulsively, like doing something without thinking it through, outside of interrupting or blurting out answers?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, aside from the talking part we just went over, I can't really think of anything. I don't really make rash decisions or just... jump into doing things out of the blue without thinking. I pretty much just stick to my routine cleaning the building at work, and then I go home. So, no, I wouldn't say I act impulsively in other ways.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty paying attention to details or making careless mistakes in your work or other activities?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I just do what needs to be done, so... yeah, missing details isn't really a problem for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble organizing tasks and activities?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I have trouble with that. Like, on my shifts—I work as a janitor—I just go from mopping to checking the trash to straightening chairs without really stopping. I just keep moving. \n\nAnd even at home, when you'd think I'd want to rest my feet... I'll get back and just start puttering around in the garage, or I'll be re-sorting my tools instead of actually sitting down after work. It's just hard to turn it off.\n\nDoctor: Did you have trouble sitting still or felt overly restless when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I did. I remember getting in trouble back in grade school quite a bit for it, actually. I was always popping out of my seat, or... well, talking over the teacher. I'd usually just rush ahead and start on things before the directions were even finished. My mother, she used to say I acted like I was wound too tight, even as a kid. So it's definitely not a new thing.\n\nDoctor: Did you often interrupt others or blurt out answers before they finished speaking when you were in school?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I did, and honestly, I still do. It happens a lot at work or when I'm volunteering at church. When my supervisor or one of the church staff starts giving me instructions, I just... jump in. I think I know what they're going to say, so I blurt it out before they can even finish their sentence. The problem is, half the time I get it wrong, and then they have to stop and correct me. It's frustrating for them, and it's frustrating for me, but I just can't seem to hold it in.\n\nDoctor: Do you often intrude on others or interrupt their activities?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so. I mean, I know I interrupt when people are talking, like I just mentioned, but I don't usually barge in on what they're actually doing. \n\nAt work, I'm mostly just doing my own thing, cleaning and moving around, so I don't really get in anyone's way or take over their tasks. And at home I'm usually out in the garage by myself. So, no, I haven't really noticed myself doing that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been known to act impulsively, like doing something without thinking it through, outside of interrupting or blurting out answers?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, aside from the talking part we just went over, I can't really think of anything. I don't really make rash decisions or just... jump into doing things out of the blue without thinking. I pretty much just stick to my routine cleaning the building at work, and then I go home. So, no, I wouldn't say I act impulsively in other ways.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty paying attention to details or making careless mistakes in your work or other activities?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My job is pretty straightforward, you know? Just cleaning and keeping the building in order. I've got my routine down, so I don't really miss things or make careless mistakes. I just do what needs to be done, so... yeah, missing details isn't really a problem for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble organizing tasks and activities?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I have trouble with that. Like I said, my job is pretty routine. I know what I need to do every day—empty the trash, mop the floors, check the bathrooms—and I just do it. I don't really have to plan out big projects or organize much. I just go down my list and get it done. So, organizing hasn't really been an issue for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often talk excessively, even when it's not your turn or when others are trying to converse?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I talk excessively. I know we talked earlier about how I can sometimes blurt things out or interrupt, but I don't just go on and on. Mostly I'm pretty quiet. I just keep my head down and do my cleaning at work, so I'm not really talking people's ears off or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms, such as restlessness and interrupting others, caused you any significant problems in your relationships or at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it actually has at work. Um... I've started getting some complaints and corrections lately. I guess I end up missing parts of instructions when people are talking to me. And it creates some friction, you know, especially during room turnovers or when we're trying to make decisions about supplies. It actually got to the point where my supervisor pushed me toward the employee assistance program. So... yeah, it's definitely causing some issues on the job.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms, such as restlessness and interrupting others, caused you any significant problems in your personal relationships?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Um, my personal life is pretty quiet. I don't really have any big issues with family or friends over it. I guess the people who know me are just kind of used to how I am by now. So, no, it hasn't really caused any major problems outside of work.\n\nDoctor: As a child, were you often described as being physically overactive, such as running, climbing, or being unable to play quietly?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I was. Um, I remember getting in trouble back in grade school for just popping out of my seat or talking over the teacher. I'd always be rushing ahead before the directions were even finished. My mother actually used to say I acted like I was wound too tight, even as a kid. So, yeah, that definitely sounds like me back then.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your restlessness or interrupting has caused you a lot of distress or anxiety?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, it gets frustrating at work when I mess up or people get annoyed with me, but I wouldn't say it causes me a lot of distress or anxiety. It's just... kind of how I've always been, so I don't really stress over it too much.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble keeping your workspace or living space organized?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say so. Um, I clean buildings for a living, so keeping things tidy and put away is just kind of second nature to me at this point. My own place is pretty organized, too. So, no, that's not really an issue I deal with.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been fired or demoted from a job because of your restlessness or difficulty following instructions?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't been fired or demoted. But... well, it has started causing some real issues lately. I've been getting complaints and having to make corrections at work because I end up missing parts of the instructions. It creates friction with the team, especially during room turnovers or when we're trying to figure out supply decisions. So, I still have my position, but it's definitely causing some trouble for me there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your symptoms, such as restlessness and interrupting, have significantly impacted your ability to function in your daily life or maintain relationships?\n\nPatient: Well, no, not significantly. It hasn't ruined any friendships or caused any big blowups at home or anything like that. It's really starting to become a problem for me.\n\nDoctor: Does this restlessness happen only in situations where you're supposed to sit still, or does it occur at other times as well?\n\nPatient: No, it's pretty much all the time. Like, on my shifts—I work as a janitor—I just go from mopping to checking the trash to straightening chairs without really stopping. I just keep moving. \n\nAnd even at home, when you'd think I'd want to rest my feet... I'll get back and just start puttering around in the garage, or I'll be re-sorting my tools instead of actually sitting down after work. It's just hard to turn it off.\n\nDoctor: Did you have trouble sitting still or felt overly restless when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I did. I remember getting in trouble back in grade school quite a bit for it, actually. I was always popping out of my seat, or... well, talking over the teacher. I'd usually just rush ahead and start on things before the directions were even finished. My mother, she used to say I acted like I was wound too tight, even as a kid. So it's definitely not a new thing.\n\nDoctor: Did you often interrupt others or blurt out answers before they finished speaking when you were in school?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I did, and honestly, I still do. It happens a lot at work or when I'm volunteering at church. When my supervisor or one of the church staff starts giving me instructions, I just... jump in. I think I know what they're going to say, so I blurt it out before they can even finish their sentence. The problem is, half the time I get it wrong, and then they have to stop and correct me. It's frustrating for them, and it's frustrating for me, but I just can't seem to hold it in.\n\nDoctor: Do you often intrude on others or interrupt their activities?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so. I mean, I know I interrupt when people are talking, like I just mentioned, but I don't usually barge in on what they're actually doing. \n\nAt work, I'm mostly just doing my own thing, cleaning and moving around, so I don't really get in anyone's way or take over their tasks. And at home I'm usually out in the garage by myself. So, no, I haven't really noticed myself doing that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been known to act impulsively, like doing something without thinking it through, outside of interrupting or blurting out answers?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, aside from the talking part we just went over, I can't really think of anything. I don't really make rash decisions or just... jump into doing things out of the blue without thinking. I pretty much just stick to my routine cleaning the building at work, and then I go home. So, no, I wouldn't say I act impulsively in other ways.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty paying attention to details or making careless mistakes in your work or other activities?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My job is pretty straightforward, you know? Just cleaning and keeping the building in order. I've got my routine down, so I don't really miss things or make careless mistakes. I just do what needs to be done, so... yeah, missing details isn't really a problem for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble organizing tasks and activities?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I have trouble with that. Like I said, my job is pretty routine. I know what I need to do every day—empty the trash, mop the floors, check the bathrooms—and I just do it. I don't really have to plan out big projects or organize much. I just go down my list and get it done. So, organizing hasn't really been an issue for me.\n\nDoctor: Do you often talk excessively, even when it's not your turn or when others are trying to converse?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I talk excessively. I know we talked earlier about how I can sometimes blurt things out or interrupt, but I don't just go on and on. Mostly I'm pretty quiet. I just keep my head down and do my cleaning at work, so I'm not really talking people's ears off or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms, such as restlessness and interrupting others, caused you any significant problems in your relationships or at work?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it actually has at work. Um... I've started getting some complaints and corrections lately. I guess I end up missing parts of instructions when people are talking to me. And it creates some friction, you know, especially during room turnovers or when we're trying to make decisions about supplies. It actually got to the point where my supervisor pushed me toward the employee assistance program. So... yeah, it's definitely causing some issues on the job.\n\nDoctor: Have your symptoms, such as restlessness and interrupting others, caused you any significant problems in your personal relationships?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Um, my personal life is pretty quiet. I don't really have any big issues with family or friends over it. I guess the people who know me are just kind of used to how I am by now. So, no, it hasn't really caused any major problems outside of work.\n\nDoctor: As a child, were you often described as being physically overactive, such as running, climbing, or being unable to play quietly?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I was. Um, I remember getting in trouble back in grade school for just popping out of my seat or talking over the teacher. I'd always be rushing ahead before the directions were even finished. My mother actually used to say I acted like I was wound too tight, even as a kid. So, yeah, that definitely sounds like me back then.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your restlessness or interrupting has caused you a lot of distress or anxiety?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, it gets frustrating at work when I mess up or people get annoyed with me, but I wouldn't say it causes me a lot of distress or anxiety. It's just... kind of how I've always been, so I don't really stress over it too much.\n\nDoctor: Do you often have trouble keeping your workspace or living space organized?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say so. Um, I clean buildings for a living, so keeping things tidy and put away is just kind of second nature to me at this point. My own place is pretty organized, too. So, no, that's not really an issue I deal with.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been fired or demoted from a job because of your restlessness or difficulty following instructions?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't been fired or demoted. But... well, it has started causing some real issues lately. I've been getting complaints and having to make corrections at work because I end up missing parts of the instructions. It creates friction with the team, especially during room turnovers or when we're trying to figure out supply decisions. It actually got to the point recently where my supervisor pushed me toward the employee assistance program. So, I still have my position, but it's definitely causing some trouble for me there.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like your symptoms, such as restlessness and interrupting, have significantly impacted your ability to function in your daily life or maintain relationships?\n\nPatient: Well, no, not significantly. I mean, I just told you about the issues at work with my team, so there's that. But outside of my job? My daily life is pretty normal. I get my errands done, I take care of my place just fine. \n\nAnd as for relationships... It hasn't ruined any friendships or caused any big blowups at home or anything like that. I don't really have to sit and focus on one thing for very long. It makes me really antsy. I get a bit short with people, and I'll usually start pacing or just push to get moving before it's really my turn. Just standing around waiting is pretty tough for me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I just feel like I've got this motor humming right under my skin whenever I'm supposed to sit still. \n\nLike, if I'm in a staff meeting at work, or sitting through church event briefings, I just can't stay put. I remember getting in trouble back in grade school quite a bit for it, actually. I was always popping out of my seat, or... well, talking over the teacher. Not really, no. I know I just said I hate waiting around at work, but traffic isn't a big deal for me. I usually just take the bus anyway, so I'm not the one driving. 

And when I'm talking to people... I don't think I get overly impatient. I just let them finish what they're saying. It's mostly just when I'm trying to physically get my work done that I get antsy, so I haven't really noticed it in those other kinds of situations.
